Correction (2026-09-28, answers the gpt-5.6-sol review of the AC-403 legs checkpoint): AC-403 back to PENDING and the PL-AUTH box unticked. BLOCKER (owner decision): the spec asks outside programs to use a Clerk machine token (M2M audience); the built, live design is a Clerk API key owned by a linked staff person, and a machine token has no staff person, so its workspace, role and "who did it" need an owner rule. Keep person-owned keys and change the spec wording, or add machine tokens with that rule. Every other PL-AUTH leg passes (AC-028, AC-151, AC-210, AC-212, AC-372). The three workforce comments that still described the removed sign-in-id path now say self-service follows the linked staff profile only.
Completed (2026-09-28, PL-AUTH AC-403 legs checkpoint 1): self-service leg - Delivery startTransit / confirmDelivery and PrepTask release compared a staff profile id with the sign-in id, so the assigned driver could never start or finish their own delivery and a cook could never release a task they claimed; both now check the linked staff profile (user.personId). 13 workforce own-work guards (assignment, availability, shift, time) lost the "no profile and person id equals sign-in id" fallback. Shared-event leg checked, no change: Event, EventDish, timeline activity and event numbers are readable by all staff; Event changes stay with event and sales roles. Machine leg checked, no change: outside programs use a Clerk API key owned by a linked Capsule person, turned into that person's sign-in and forwarded to the same generated command dispatcher (tests/agent/capsule-api-key-gateway.test.ts, tests/proofs/command-api-identity.runtime.test.ts); keys not owned by a user are refused. The spec says "Clerk M2M audience"; the built design is person-owned keys (person-first auth needs a staff profile) - spec wording to align, not a code gap. Proof: self-service-person-link 3/3 (fails on old code); shift-lifecycle now signs in through a linked profile. Manifest-source porting: needed. AC-151 cached-client leg: new tests/proofs/cached-client-role-change - a signed-in device keeps the same token with owner claims; after an admin changes the person to kitchen staff the next client list is empty, a known client id reads as missing and a new client is refused (no code change: access already follows the staff profile on every call). All six acceptance rows (AC-028, AC-151, AC-210, AC-212, AC-372, AC-403) now PASS; PL-AUTH box ticked.
Completed (2026-09-28, PL-AUTH provider state, AC-372, answers the gpt-5.6-sol review): IntegrationConnection markConnected / recordFailure / recordSyncSuccess are private (server-only) steps, and authorize no longer takes the provider account id or scopes; a new private linkAccount step records the account Stripe created. convex/stripeConnect.ts records Stripe's own answer through the internal recordStripeAnswer mutation as the tenant system identity (other workspace's connection refused). authorize / reauthorize / disconnect stay admin actions. Proof: caller-sent-totals 4/4 (an owner calling the four steps by hand is refused and the row is untouched; the server path links, connects and records a failure). Manifest-source porting: needed (private markers, authorize params, linkAccount). Still open in PL-AUTH: AC-403 legs, AC-151 cached-client leg.
Completed (2026-09-27, PL-AUTH caller-sent totals checkpoint, AC-372): ROOT CAUSE - a Manifest `private command` (a step only the server runs) was still a public Convex mutation, so any signed-in person could call it with numbers of their own; the regen patch (scripts/apply-own-workspace-links.ts) now makes every private step refuse all callers but the tenant system identity (convex/lib/serverOnlyStep.ts), reactions unaffected. Private now: Invoice applyPayment / applyCredit / recordCreditMemo (no balance lowered without a settled payment or issued credit memo), VendorOrder syncLineTotals (no typed subtotal under the spend-approval limit), EventCloseout followEventCommercial, IngredientDemand syncFromContributions / alignPurchasingWeek (already private, now enforced). VendorOrder updateTotals takes only tax and shipping; the subtotal stays the line sum. Invoice issue with lines: line, tax-per-rate, subtotal and tax money must equal what the server works out from the lines, the workspace tax rates and the client's tax-exempt setting (convex/lib/invoiceIssueTotals.ts; first issue only; single-amount invoices from the approval cascade and imports unchanged). Invoice followEventPrice takes the event's own quoted price (no amount param). Proposal followEventHeadcount stays a person action (readiness list) but must use the event's count, and its money must equal the central calc for its lines or stay unchanged with no lines (convex/lib/proposalFollowTotals.ts). Readiness "correct it with a credit" now points at CreditMemo.issue. Staff entry by design, no change: Proposal draft money (lines are server-priced by draftProposalWithLines / recompute), RevenueAttribution apply event revenue (operator confirms the estimate), order tax/shipping, Payment markMatched / updateProviderTransactionIds / verify / dispute (the manual reconciliation desk), Message / MessageThread provider ids (staff paste messages; no live mail provider). Not wired (noted, not changed): Invoice.recordRefund has no caller - Payment.refund does not reach the invoice. Proof: caller-sent-totals 3/3 (fails on old code); vendor-order-pricing-totals and reconciliation-flags-readiness updated to the new refusals; 49 related files pass; typecheck, format:check pass. Manifest-source porting: needed (private markers, updateTotals params, followEventPrice, followEventHeadcount constraint, InvoiceIssued newlyIssued). Still open in PL-AUTH: AC-403 legs, AC-151 cached-client leg.
Completed (2026-09-27, ninth review answer): a retry that uses a renamed tenant-shared/ key now also finds the receipt the earlier version saved under the same key without the prefix (import, inbox thread/message/qualify, card payment record/settle all sent unprefixed keys before), and refuses it with the earlier-version guidance - never replayed, never re-run. Proof: import-resume-fault-injection (old venue receipt: that venue is not created again and waits in the review queue with the guidance), stripe-outbox-dedupe (old record and old settle receipts: no payment recorded or settled twice, invoice balance untouched), idempotency-replay-scope (old inbox thread, message and lead receipts: no second thread, message or lead); 3 files / 15 tests pass, typecheck pass. Still open in PL-AUTH: caller-sent totals and provider state (AC-372), AC-403 legs, AC-151 cached-client leg.
Completed (2026-09-27, eighth review answer): the idempotency reservation is stable per workspace and step again, whoever retries. The per-person receipt key made a different authorized operator (or the same operator after an access change) miss the cache and re-execute the step, so import crash recovery, inbox ingestion and Stripe sync could duplicate records. convex/lib/commandIdempotency.ts now splits the two jobs: the receipt key is workspace + step + retry key (never the caller), and the caller scope saved beside the answer (the row's command field after a |) decides what a hit returns - the same caller with the same access replays the full answer; any other caller is refused with guidance, without revealing the answer or running the step again; a retry key starting with tenant-shared/ claims a tenant-workflow receipt that another signed-in caller in the same workspace may use to recover the original record reference (never the saved answer) - import resume, inbox ingestion and Stripe sync now send such keys. Receipts from the pre-reservation versions are still refused with guidance, never replayed or re-executed. Proof: idempotency-replay-scope 6/6 (adds a same-workspace second-operator refusal with no re-run, and a crash-window handoff where operator B recovers operator A's original record reference, not the answer); import-resume-fault-injection and stripe-outbox-dedupe updated to the shared keys; full proof suite 181 files / 492 tests pass, tenant-access-matrix 2/2 (0 leaks), typecheck and format:check pass. Still open in PL-AUTH: caller-sent totals and provider state (AC-372), AC-403 legs, AC-151 cached-client leg.
Completed (2026-09-27, seventh review answer): four closes on the saved-answer cache, the link check and the assistant key. (1) Receipts saved by the old single-key cache cannot be attributed to a caller, so a retry that hits one is refused with guidance ("used by an earlier version ... send again with a new key") instead of replayed or re-executed - no duplicate work, no cross-caller leak; the refused step's record is untouched. (2) The own-workspace link check now runs AFTER the replay lookup (the regen patch anchors it before the delegated run call, or after the replay prologue in inline createVia bodies), so a retry gets its saved answer even when a linked record was deleted since. (3) Link inputs the Manifest declares as ref / belongsTo fields are now checked against their exact table - malformed, wrong-table, missing and other-workspace ids all get the same "A linked record was not found" (entity-to-table names resolved from schema.ts at regen with a loud failure on ambiguity; Person maps to people). Bare uuid fields stay lenient: a value that resolves to no record anywhere is an outside id and passes. (4) The assistant settings and company key follow the workspace the sign-in claims: convex/assistantConfig.ts picks the live Person row of the claimed workspace (hint from tenantIdFromIdentityClaims), so a profile removed from A but live in B presenting A claims is refused, while B claims still work. (5) ShareLink create/revoke record user.personId (the staff profile) not user.id, refuse unlinked sign-ins with a plain message, and a proof checks the stored references resolve to real Person rows. Proofs: idempotency-replay-scope 5/5 (new pre-upgrade receipt and replay-before-link cases), assistant-config-removed-person 3/3 (new inactive-A/live-B claims case), create-server-owned-fields 4/4 (new share-link person case); four proposal/share-link proofs updated to link staff profiles. Still open: same as below.

Completed (2026-09-27, sixth review answer): a sign-in still linked to a removed staff profile fails closed even when it still carries the company's workspace and role claims (a removed organization member keeps those claims). getAuthContext (convex/lib/authContext.ts) answers anonymous for such a sign-in before any IdP fallback, so it can neither run a step fresh nor save or replay a saved answer; only a sign-in never linked in that workspace still bootstraps from its claims. The proofs now give the removed identities those claims: idempotency-replay-scope proves a removed profile (inactive and deleted) with owner claims neither replays nor re-runs, while a never-linked sign-in with the same claims still sets up; the matrix's removed person of workspace A now carries workspace A's owner claims and stays refused (both world copies keep the caller's own staff profile, so both copies see the same caller). idempotency-replay-scope 3/3; matrix 2/2 (919 reads / 965 writes x outsiders, 27275 + 31875 probes, no leak). Still open: same as below.

Completed (2026-09-27, fifth review answer): a saved step answer replays only for the same person with the same access. The saved answer is returned before the step's role checks, so a same-workspace caller without the role (sales asking for a kitchen answer) or a removed person with a known key got it; and the key was worked out again after the step ran, so a person who removed their own profile saved their record under the signed-out scope. The key now carries workspace, sign-in, staff profile, role and switched-off areas, is worked out once before the step runs, and a signed-out caller or a sign-in with no workspace saves and replays nothing. The link check now looks only at inputs the Manifest declares as record links (uuid or list of uuid, or a field of the record's ref / belongsTo link), so text, codes and outside-system ids are never refused. idempotency-replay-scope 3/3; matrix retarget 1275 probes, 0 leaks. Still open: same as below.

Completed (2026-09-27, fourth review answer): a saved step answer replays only for the same workspace and step. Every generated step looked up its saved answer by the caller's retry key alone, before checking its record, so workspace B or a signed-out caller with workspace A's record id and a known key got A's saved answer (for an ingredient, A's whole record). Every generated step now saves and looks up its answer under a key scoped to the caller's workspace and the step (convex/lib/commandIdempotency.ts, added by the same regen patch); team chat send uses the same key. idempotency-replay-scope 1/1 (A's retry replays; B, signed-out, and a missing record with a known key are refused; B's own record with A's key runs fresh). Still open: same as below.

Completed (2026-09-27, third review answer): a caller may only link records of its own workspace. The recipe seed checked its stored links, then wrote the links the caller sent; now the links sent must be its own (recipe-seed-own-links 1/1). A new matrix probe (workspace B changes its OWN record while every other link names workspace A) found the same cause in 56 generated steps (dish task materials, prep comments, event drafts, share links, venue terms and more): they stored another workspace's record ids. Every public generated mutation now refuses a record id of another workspace, or of a missing record, with the same answer before it runs (convex/lib/ownWorkspaceLinks.ts, added by the regen patch scripts/apply-own-workspace-links.ts); retarget probe 1275 probes, 0 leaks. Six tests that expected such links to be stored and refused later now expect the refusal at once and keep the later check with directly written old data. Still open: same as below.

Completed (2026-09-27, second review answer): the PL-AUTH matrix aims every link name at workspace A (LINK_TABLE; an unmapped *Id / *Ids name fails the test), runs the public quote form in both copies, sends changed values in outside writes, checks A after each accepted outside write and compares each write's answer with the copy without A (27275 read probes, 31875 write probes, no leak; owner reach 847/919 reads, 254/965 writes). It found and fixed a real gap: anyone, even signed out, could start a recipe seed (EventDishComponentSeed seed), and one aimed at another workspace's recipe part read its recipe lines; seed now needs manage, sales or kitchen access and an event, dish line and recipe part of the caller's workspace. The assistant turn refuses removed or unlinked callers before the fallback AI key. Still open: same as below (caller-sent totals and provider state, AC-403 legs, AC-151 cached-client leg).

Completed (2026-09-27, review answer): the PL-AUTH matrix now compares every read's answer (value or error text) with the same call in a copy with NO workspace A records, so leaked names, numbers, counts, flags and found/not-found differences all fail; both workspaces are fully seeded with links to their own records (A numbers 7331, B numbers 1); workspace B roles are linked staff profiles plus one unlinked sign-in; each optional argument naming a record or workspace is also sent alone; tests/proofs/tenant-access-matrix.ledger.json records per function whether A's owner reaches A (833/919 reads, 244/965 writes) or the owner's own result as the reason, and a reaching entry must keep reaching. It found and fixed a real gap: the in-app assistant settings and company AI key followed any not-deleted staff profile, so a removed person still saw them (convex/assistantConfig.ts now uses the sign-in live-person rule; assistant-config-removed-person 1/1). PurchaseNeed create applies its new-need defaults only to a new need (create-server-owned-fields replay proof 3/3). By design: re-owning a browser's phone alerts needs its secret address. Still open: same as below.

Completed (2026-09-27): the **PL-AUTH workspace/role matrix and server-owned create fields checkpoint** is proven; the PL-AUTH box stays unchecked and AC-151, AC-210, AC-212, AC-372 and AC-403 stay PENDING. Matrix (AC-151 / PR12-10, AC-212, AC-403 not-found): new tests/proofs/tenant-access-matrix.runtime.test.ts puts one workspace A record in each of the 162 workspace-owned tables, then runs all 919 public reads and 965 public writes in convex/ as each of the 22 roles of workspace B, a signed-out caller and a removed person of workspace A, with arguments pointing at workspace A's ids, parent ids and workspace id: no read returns a workspace A record, a workspace A id answers exactly like a missing id, no write adds, changes or removes a workspace A record (workspace A's owner hits its records in 604 reads and changes 95 records with the same probes). This replaces the proposed AC-212 static scan. By design, checked not skipped: the website quote form gives anyone only the names of active service styles and occasions (it serves the first active company, per its code note "single-org deployment today"); turning phone alerts off by device address needs the phone's own secret address. Real gap (AC-372): the create steps of RevenueAttribution, ShareLink, ClientPortalLink, PurchaseNeed, MessageThread and QuoteSubmission took approval, applied amounts, revoke/view tracking, order links, status and processing results from the caller; each create now sets them itself and no longer accepts them. Still open on PL-AUTH: AC-372 caller-sent totals and provider state on other steps (Invoice issue, Proposal draft, VendorOrder updateTotals, Payment/Message provider ids) need a review of which are staff entries by design; AC-403 self-service Person relationship, machine sign-in audience and shared-event read vs management write legs; the cached-client leg of AC-151.

Completed (2026-09-27): the **PL-AUTH AC-210 / AC-372 import match actor slice** is proven; the PL-AUTH box stays unchecked and AC-210 and AC-372 stay PENDING. Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-388 readiness flags slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-388 stays PENDING. Found a real gap: the invoice, proposal and closeout reconciliations keep a record a person changed, sent, accepted or finalized and write a flag (invoice_review, invoice_change_required, proposal_review, proposal_change_required, closeout_review, closeout_change_required) on the section 8.2 receipt, but nothing read those receipts, so nobody in the office saw that a sent bill, a seen proposal or a finalized closeout still had the old price, count or budget (section 8.3: the operator sees the affected exception). Now `convex/lib/reconciliationFlags.ts` reads the receipts of each of those three domains for the event (one indexed range read on the exact receipt keys; `requestKey` is now exported from `convex/lib/materializationReceipt.ts`) and takes the newest one written for the value the event holds now, and `convex/eventReadiness.ts` passes the still-open flags to the live projection, which shows each as a warning in plain words (bills and proposals under commercial, closeouts under closeout) with a command that is legal in the record status now: a draft bill that can still take the price gets `Invoice.followEventPrice`, a draft with tax, a discount, a payment or a credit gets `Invoice.markVoided`; a sent, viewed or overdue bill gets `Invoice.markVoided`, a part-paid bill `Invoice.applyCredit`, a paid bill `Invoice.recordCreditMemo`; a hand-sized draft proposal gets `Proposal.followEventHeadcount`, a sent or viewed proposal `Proposal.supersede`, an accepted proposal a change draft (`Proposal.draft` with replacesProposalId, via startProposalChange); a captured draft closeout gets `EventCloseout.capture`; a finalized closeout gets `Event.correctCommercial`. A flag is open only while the event still holds the price, count or budget that raised it and the flagged record is unchanged since (same version, not deleted or voided); an accepted proposal flag also goes once a live change draft replaces it. No new stored state, no new guard. This is a retry: the first attempt (fix-20260926T1030-plan-next) was rejected because it advertised commands the records could not take (capture on a finalized closeout, supersede on an accepted proposal, draft for a draft proposal, void for a review warning) and never proved proposals; it also read only the receipt head, so a price that went up, back and up again hid the sent-bill flag (the reconciliation finds its earlier receipt and writes nothing). P: `tests/proofs/reconciliation-flags-readiness.runtime.test.ts` (6 tests), each running the advertised command for real: a sent bill shows commercial.invoice_change_required with Invoice.markVoided, the flag goes when the price moves back, shows again on a return to the new price, and goes once the bill is voided; a draft bill with a deposit shows commercial.invoice_review with Invoice.followEventPrice and the flag goes once it moves to 5200; an untouched draft that followed shows none; a captured draft closeout shows closeout.closeout_review with EventCloseout.capture and goes after a new capture; a finalized closeout shows closeout.closeout_change_required with Event.correctCommercial and goes when the budget is set back; a hand-sized draft, a sent and an accepted proposal show their flags with Proposal.followEventHeadcount, Proposal.supersede and Proposal.draft, and all go after those run. With the readiness projection, final readiness, readiness summary, event overview, invoice pricing, proposal headcount, closeout follow and proof registry tests 51/51; typecheck and prettier check pass. Product gap found, not built here: no command corrects a finalized closeout (transition finalized to []), so the only legal settle is on the event; an owner decision on a closeout correction record is needed. Still open on AC-388: Final Lock answers and documents auto-create/provenance.

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-388 closeout draft slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-388 stays PENDING. Found a real money gap: closing an event seeds one draft closeout (zero actuals, budget from the event), but `Event.correctCommercial` (allowed at completed and closed_out) never touched it, so after a manager corrected the quote or budget the draft closeout finance works from kept the old budgeted revenue and cost. Now a new governed command `EventCloseout.followEventCommercial` (src/finance/event-closeout.manifest; guards: draft, not deleted, zero actual revenue, cost and headcount) moves the budget and the two variances, and a new seam `convex/lib/closeoutCommercialReconciliation.ts` (called from `convex/lib/operationalEvents.ts` on EventCommercialCorrected) uses it only for a draft still in that zero-actual shape and writes one section 8.2 closeout receipt (invoice pattern: only closeouts whose budget differs are in scope, id + version in the receipt key, prior receipt checked before any write). It runs as the tenant system role, because a sales manager may correct the budget but closeouts are finance / event-manager gated. A draft finance already captured keeps its numbers and is flagged `closeout_review`; a finalized closeout is never rewritten and is flagged `closeout_change_required`. P: new `tests/proofs/closeout-draft-commercial-follow.runtime.test.ts` (4 tests): the untouched draft follows 4500/3000 to 6000/4000 (same closeout, variances match, actuals stay 0) with one receipt; replaying the same correction changes nothing and writes no second receipt; a sales manager correcting 6000, 4500, 6000 leaves the draft on 6000; a captured draft and a finalized closeout stay at 4500 and are flagged. With the finalized-closeout, completion-correction, closeout lifecycle, terminal meanings, invoice pricing and proof registry tests 19/19; typecheck, format:check, check:closeout-manifest and check:event-manifest pass. Not done here: `convex/_generated/api.d.ts` does not list the new seam file (the maker does not run convex codegen; the file holds no Convex functions). Still open on AC-388: Final Lock answers and documents auto-create/provenance.

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-388 proposal draft slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-388 stays PENDING. Found a real gap: when the event guest count changed, an unsent draft proposal linked to the event kept the old count and the old per-guest price, so sales could send the client a quote for the wrong number of guests (the §1.3 rule says proposal drafts follow the event facts). Now a new governed command `Proposal.followEventHeadcount` (src/sales/proposal.manifest; draft only, drafted, not deleted, linked to an event, total = subtotal + tax - discount) moves the draft, and `convex/lib/proposalReconciliation.ts` (already run on EventHeadcountChanged) uses it only for a draft still on the old event count, prices it with the central calc (`src/lib/pricing.ts`: per-person lines scale, flat lines, tax and discount stay; a draft with no priced lines keeps its typed money) and restamps the line amounts through the existing `recomputeProposalTotals`. It runs as the tenant system role, because event staff may change the guest count but proposals are sales-gated. A draft a person sized to another count is their override: it keeps its count and is flagged `proposal_review` until they set it back to the event count; a change draft for an accepted proposal and a draft whose new price would go below zero are also kept and flagged. Sent, viewed and accepted proposals are never rewritten and are all flagged `proposal_change_required`. The receipt now follows the invoice pattern: only open proposals whose count differs are in scope, their id + version are in the receipt key, and the earlier receipt is checked before anything is written (a first attempt was rejected because sent proposals were not flagged and a return to an earlier count could skip a new flag). P: `tests/proofs/proposal-draft-headcount-follow.runtime.test.ts` (5 tests): an untouched draft moves 40 to 60 guests, 1450 to 2050, the per-guest line 1200 to 1800, with one receipt (updated 1); replaying 60 changes nothing and writes no second receipt; 40, 60, 40, 60 leaves the draft on 60; a draft sized 50 by a person stays 50 and is flagged, sent and viewed proposals stay at 40 and are flagged, an accepted proposal stays and is flagged; a draft added after an earlier visit to 60 is flagged when the count returns to 60 while the other draft still follows. With the headcount/proposal, proposal central-path, proposal projection, headcount menu/packet, override-survival, invoice pricing and proof registry tests 25/25; typecheck, format:check, check:event-manifest and check:commercial-manifest pass. Still open on AC-388: Final Lock answers, documents and closeout auto-create/provenance.

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-388 invoice draft slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-388 stays PENDING (AC-037, AC-191, AC-390 and AC-407 are PASS, so AC-388 is the last open row). Found a real money gap: after approval the event has one unsent draft invoice made from the quoted price, but `Event.changePricing` (allowed at planning, pending_approval and approved) never touched it, so the draft kept the old price and finance could send the client a bill for the wrong amount. Now a new governed command `Invoice.followEventPrice` (src/sales/invoice-core.manifest; guards: draft, not sent, nothing paid, no tax, discount or credit) moves the draft to the new price, and a new seam `convex/lib/invoicePricingReconciliation.ts` (called from `convex/lib/operationalEvents.ts` on EventPricingChanged) uses it only for a draft still in its auto-made shape and writes one §8.2 invoice receipt. A draft that finance changed (deposit, tax, discount, line items, payment, credit) keeps its money and is flagged `invoice_review`; a sent or later invoice is never rewritten and is flagged `invoice_change_required`. P: new `tests/proofs/pricing-invoice-reconciliation.runtime.test.ts` (5 tests): the untouched draft follows 4500 to 5200 with the same id and number and one receipt; replaying 5200 changes nothing and writes no second receipt; 5200, 4500, 5200 leaves the draft at 5200; a draft with a deposit stays 4500 and is flagged; a sent invoice stays sent at 4500 and is flagged. That file 5/5 with the sent-invoice, reaction-replay, invoice lifecycle, approved-ensure, finance-cancellation, completion-correction and proof registry tests (20/20); typecheck and format:check pass. Not done here: `convex/_generated/api.d.ts` does not list the new seam file yet (the maker could not run convex codegen; the file holds no Convex functions, so types are not affected; the next codegen adds it). Still open on AC-388: proposal draft, Final Lock answers, documents and closeout auto-create/provenance.

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-191 web push slice** is proven, and AC-191 is now PASS; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked (AC-037, AC-388, AC-390 and AC-407 stay open). No code defect was found. P: new `tests/proofs/push-outbox-dedupe.runtime.test.ts` (9 tests, web-push mocked): run-of-show phone alerts (`convex/runOfShowAlerts.ts` + `convex/runOfShowAlertsSend.ts`) are pushed once per device across three scans with one RunAlertSent ledger row; a push that no device took (503 on every device) writes no sent row and is sent once on the next scan; a device the push service reports gone (410) is retired and never pushed again; a scan from a loop that was turned off and on again sends nothing while the new loop sends once; a crew list that names a person of another tenant pushes only the own-tenant device. Team chat push (`convex/teamChatPushSend.ts`) pushes each recipient device once and never the sender devices; `teamChatSend.sendWithFiles` resent with the same draft key returns the same message and schedules one push, not two; a message addressed to another tenant person pushes nothing; a message older than five minutes wakes no phone. That file 9/9 with the proof registry gate and the SMS and webhook outbox proofs (23/23); typecheck pass; format:check pass. Found, not fixed: a chat push that fails on every device (network or 5xx) is not tried again (it is a one-hour ping, so this is left as is); and the run-alert on/off ledger picks the older row when an off and an on land in the same millisecond (`convex/lib/runOfShowAlertLoop.ts` sorts by createdAt only), which a person clicking can not hit.

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-191 invoice reminder email slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-191 stays PENDING. Writing the proof found a real money defect: `convex/invoiceReminders.ts` made the payment link in each reminder email on the Capsule platform Stripe account (no `Stripe-Account` header), so a client who paid from a reminder paid Capsule, not the caterer (against issue #112), and the invoice payment sync, which reads sessions on the caterer account, never found that payment. The paid-check before each reminder had the same fault, so a client who paid from an invoice payment link kept getting reminders. Now reminders use the same caterer-account lookup and headers as `convex/invoicePayments.ts` (`requireConnectedAccountId`, `connectedAccountHeaders`, now exported) for both the link and the paid-check, and turning on reminders asks the workspace to connect Stripe first (a disconnected workspace gets a failed reminder that is tried again, never a Capsule-account charge). P: new `tests/proofs/invoice-reminder-outbox-dedupe.runtime.test.ts` (4 tests, 3 failed before the fix): repeated runs of one reminder date send one email and make one link on the caterer account; a client who pays through the reminder link is recorded once by the payment sync and gets no later reminder; a failed email send is tried again under the same email key and then sent once with no second link; a workspace that disconnected Stripe gets no Stripe call at all and another workspace can not send or set up reminders. That file 4/4 with the webhook, SMS, calendar and Stripe outbox proofs, the proof registry gate and the finance ledger tests (29 tests); typecheck and format:check pass. Still open on AC-191: the web push legs (`convex/teamChatPushSend.ts`, `convex/runOfShowAlertsSend.ts`).

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-191 Stripe outbox slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-191 stays PENDING. No code defect was found in this leg: `convex/invoicePayments.ts` already records a paid Checkout session through `Payment.record` and `Payment.settle` with fixed per-session keys and marks the session done on the invoice ledger. P: new `tests/proofs/stripe-outbox-dedupe.runtime.test.ts` (4 tests): repeated syncs record one completed payment for one paid session, apply it to the invoice once (paid, 400 paid, 0 due) and stop asking Stripe about a session that is done; a failed Stripe lookup records nothing, is tried again on the next sync, and is then recorded once; a sync that stopped after it recorded the payment finishes on the next sync with no second payment and the invoice paid once; every Stripe call carries the workspace connected account; a finance user from another tenant can not create a link, sync, or read the link on the invoice, and no Stripe call goes out for them. That file 4/4, with the webhook, SMS and calendar outbox proofs and the proof registry gate 22/22; typecheck and format:check pass. Still open on AC-191: the invoice reminder email leg (`convex/invoiceReminders.ts`) and the web push legs (`convex/teamChatPushSend.ts`, `convex/runOfShowAlertsSend.ts`).

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-191 calendar outbox slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-191 stays PENDING. Writing the proof found a real defect: `reconcileTenant` in `convex/googleCalendar.ts` passed its whole argument object (including `scheduleNext`) to `loadReconciliationContext`, whose validator accepts only `tenantId` and `connectionId`, so every Google Calendar sync run (the first run after connecting, the 60-second loop, and "Sync now") failed with "Unexpected field scheduleNext" before it read one event. Now it passes only the two fields. P: new `tests/proofs/calendar-outbox-dedupe.runtime.test.ts` (4 tests, all 4 failed before the fix): repeated sync runs write an unchanged event once and record one synced row; a failed write is tried again on the next run under the same fixed Google event id (so Google never holds two copies) and then written once; one tenant's run never writes another tenant's events and a connection id from another tenant ends as disconnected; only the newest connection keeps a sync loop running (an older connection ends with no writes and no next run). That file 4/4, webhook and SMS outbox proofs and the proof registry gate pass (18 tests); typecheck and format:check pass. Still open on AC-191: the Stripe outbox leg.

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-191 SMS single-scan-chain slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-191 stays PENDING. This fixes the finding recorded in the note below: each `enableAlerts` started another self-rescheduling `scanTenant` chain that never ended while alerts stayed on, so a manager who turned alerts on twice left two 5-minute scan loops running for good, and two chains could race inside one scan window. Now `enableAlerts` puts a new chain id in the payload of its `SmsAlertsEnabled` row and passes it to `scanTenant`, which carries it on every reschedule; `loadScanContext` returns the chain id of the newest `SmsAlertsEnabled` row, and a scheduled scan whose chain id is not that one (including older chains with no id, once any chain id exists) ends with status `superseded`, no sends and no reschedule. One-off scans (`scheduleNext: false`) are unchanged, and workspaces whose enable rows carry no chain id keep the old behavior. P: new case in `tests/proofs/sms-outbox-dedupe.runtime.test.ts` "only the newest scan chain keeps running; older chains end" (old-id and no-id scans send nothing and schedule nothing; the newest chain sends the alert and schedules exactly one next scan with its own id); that file 5/5 pass; typecheck and format:check pass. Still open on AC-191: calendar and Stripe outbox legs.

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-191 SMS outbox slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-191 stays PENDING. High-urgency SMS alerts (`convex/smsAlerts.ts`) already deduped sends by trigger + person on the manifestEvents ledger, but a failed send was retried on every 5-minute scan with no limit, so a person with a bad phone number was re-texted through Twilio up to about 288 times a day while the trigger stayed live. `loadScanContext` now counts `SmsAlertFailed` rows per trigger + person and treats a key as done after three failed tries (the same bound as the webhook outbox). P: new `tests/proofs/sms-outbox-dedupe.runtime.test.ts` (4 tests: repeated scans send each alert once per person; a failing send is tried three times over five scans, then left alone; a later success after a failure is sent once and not again; one tenant's alerts never reach another tenant's people) 4/4 pass, webhook-outbox-dedupe 6/6 still pass; typecheck and format:check pass. Finding (not fixed here): each `enableAlerts` call schedules another self-rescheduling `scanTenant` chain that never ends while alerts stay on — the same duplicate-chain defect the webhook slice below fixed; the ledger dedupe keeps sends once-only between scans, but two chains can race inside one scan window. Still open on AC-191: that SMS chain fix, calendar and Stripe outbox legs.

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-191 webhook single-dispatch-chain slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-191 stays PENDING. This fixes the finding recorded in the note below: each `registerEndpoint` started another self-rescheduling webhook dispatch chain that never ended, so a workspace with several endpoints kept several background loops running every minute for good. Now `registerEndpoint` records a `WebhookDispatchChainStarted` row (new `internal.webhookIntegrations.recordChainStart`, on the existing WebhookDispatchTick ledger entity) and passes that chain id to `dispatchPending`, which carries it on every reschedule; a scheduled tick whose chain id is not the newest recorded one (including older chains with no id, once any chain id exists) ends without sending or rescheduling. One-off calls (`scheduleNext: false`) are unchanged, and workspaces with no recorded chain keep the old behavior. P: new case in `tests/proofs/webhook-outbox-dedupe.runtime.test.ts` "only the newest dispatch chain keeps running; older chains end" (old-id and no-id ticks send nothing and schedule nothing; the newest chain sends both endpoints events and schedules exactly one next tick with its own id); that file 6/6 pass; typecheck and format:check pass. Still open on AC-191: calendar, Stripe and SMS outbox legs.

Completed (2026-09-26): the **PL-RECONCILIATION AC-191 webhook outbox slice** is proven; the PL-RECONCILIATION box stays unchecked and AC-191 stays PENDING. No product code changed. New proof `tests/proofs/webhook-outbox-dedupe.runtime.test.ts` (5 tests) runs the real `internal.webhookIntegrations.dispatchPending` worker against the durable manifestEvents outbox with a stubbed HTTP call: (1) two EventApproved outbox rows are sent once each across three dispatch ticks, with exactly one WebhookDeliverySucceeded row per source event; (2) a tick inside the duplicate-chain collapse window sends nothing, and the next real tick sends only the new event; (3) an endpoint that keeps failing is tried on three later ticks (attempt 1, 2, 3 recorded as WebhookDeliveryFailed) and then left alone on ticks four and five; (4) a success after a failure is sent once and not sent again; (5) the events of one tenant never reach the endpoint of another tenant. P: that file 5/5 pass; typecheck and format:check pass. Still open on AC-191: the same once-only property for the Google Calendar sync, Stripe payment reconcile and SMS alert workers, plus the internal typed-event/reaction half beyond the existing safe-template-materialization and operational-transactions proofs. Finding recorded, not fixed: every `registerEndpoint` call schedules a new self-rescheduling dispatch chain, and the collapse branch reschedules instead of ending the extra chain, so a tenant with several registered endpoints keeps several idle chains alive (deliveries stay once-only because successes are keyed per endpoint and source event). Still open on PL-RECONCILIATION: AC-191 remainder and AC-388 remainder.

This file now serves two roles. The audit below it was plan only: it audited shared `dev` at `be4288fb`, beginning 2026-09-20, against all 22 files under `specs/` and all 115 files under `src/lib/`, with no application or test implementation. Since 2026-09-21 the plan also tracks real implementation: the first PL-BUILD increment (#380 root-count repair) is done and verified on Windows, with evidence in [build-gate-evidence-2026-09-21.md](codex-plans/whole-spec-audit-2026-09-20/build-gate-evidence-2026-09-21.md). Source hashes, stable criterion mappings, current code evidence and shared-library reuse are recorded in [source-coverage.json](codex-plans/whole-spec-audit-2026-09-20/source-coverage.json), [shared-library.md](codex-plans/whole-spec-audit-2026-09-20/shared-library.md) and [review-decisions.md](codex-plans/whole-spec-audit-2026-09-20/review-decisions.md).

The outcome is the full audience job: Josh sells once and operates from one Event; sales carries every client decision forward; Kayden can prep, pack, work and record time from a phone; clients can request, accept and pay; Tim can reconcile live money and operations. Every in-spec gap remains below. Priority orders dependencies, not a release ceiling or an owner deferral.

Preserve the working transactional booking and menu copy, quote conversion checkpoints, recipe saved review/finalization, exact unit helpers, culinary demand, shared weekly purchasing, inventory/staffing cancellation callbacks, atomic templates, live dashboards, payroll CSV, durable invoice reminders, number sequence, scoped file reads and authorized self-hosted release orchestrator. Where code exists without full proof, the task is qualification followed only by demonstrated repairs. Reuse `src/lib` and authored seams; no parallel CRM, pricing engine, command API, domain model or workflow engine.

The backend contract permits backend repairs plus minimum typed consumer compatibility, not a UI redesign. Separate older product requirements still need their normal user-facing workflows. DESIGN.md front matter controls presentation; incomplete facts create scoped issues, not global service freezes. Generated output changes only through `bun run manifest:regen`. Each future implementation task gets a narrow worker brief with exact owners, a neighboring pattern, prohibited changes and its named proving command; only one editing/testing worker runs at a time.

Open meaning decision: backend §4.2 uses `final` for service finished; §14.2 uses it for Ops Final review. Older complete-product §3.3 also distinguishes Confirmed from execution. The owner has been asked to choose. Preserve both required outcomes and plan the lifecycle mapping explicitly; proposed reconciliation is to keep the existing post-service `final` and represent pre-departure Ops Final as readiness, but this proposal is **not owner approval**. This does not block independent work. Missing authoritative manual/KPI details and business-specific financial/retention/pay terms must be obtained before asserting parity or activating their calculations; safe implementation and evidence preparation remain planned.

Acceptance is [ACCEPTANCE_TESTS.md](ACCEPTANCE_TESTS.md). A checkbox in this plan is not proof. Each task below names all its required AC IDs; the source ledger contains the precise gap/evidence per criterion. Run the named criterion tests (or extend their existing owning suite), applicable runtime/browser/J evidence, deterministic regeneration when relevant, and `bun run check`. Never create speculative implementation-only tests or restore removed source-string tests. Independent different-model APPROVE is required before merge; production release requires explicit current-conversation release authorization and the approved orchestrator.

Completed (2026-09-28, batch-20260928): company kitchens (new OperatingLocation: street, city, country, checked time zone; managers add/revise/switch off; revise bumps the version), Venue.timeZone, Event kitchen choice, company drive-time rules (safety buffer, traffic, refresh hours); server route provider (Google Routes computeRoutes, GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY) with route facts stored per attempt (kitchen/venue record + version, provider, mode, departure basis, traffic, duration, distance, fetched/expiry, request id); successful legs set travel through the server-only Event.applyRouteTravel; failure, no key, no route or an unresolved address keep travel unknown with ROUTE_REQUIRED (readiness planning.route_required), a failed refresh keeps the last fact marked stale (planning.route_stale); event changes and expiry queue an automatic check; screens: /admin/kitchens, Drive time panel on event timing, venue time zone. Found and fixed: TenantSystemCommandRunner did not raise generated reads, so a system run with no signed-in person lost the timeline read. Decisions: (1) route fact storage - (a) new Manifest entity: generated reads and reviewable fields, but its create step is public and a browser could send its own drive time; (b) server-only ledger rows (same as Stripe records): nobody can forge them, every attempt kept, but reads need an authored query; chose (b). (2) Kitchen when the event names none - (a) every event must pick: a click on every event; (b) a company default setting: one more setting to keep right; (c) the only active kitchen, ask only when there are several: no clicks for one-kitchen caterers, never guesses between two; chose (c). (3) Venue with no time zone - (a) refuse the route: a chore on every venue; (b) use the kitchen zone and say so on the panel: right for local caterers and visible; chose (b). (4) Safety buffer default 0 vs 15 minutes: 15 makes the crew leave early by default, 0 hides the rule; chose 15, editable. (5) On failure, clear hand-typed travel vs keep it: clearing loses a real person value, keeping it is not a system guess and readiness still says ROUTE_REQUIRED; chose keep. Proofs: route-fact 6/6, route-source-facts 5/5, route-backed-timeline 3/3. AC-382, AC-425, AC-426 PASS. Owners/reuse: `src/features/admin/import/ExternalRecordsReconcilePage.tsx; src/import/; convex/importCommit.ts`. Acceptance: AC-059, AC-065. Verify every named test/review on those rows; retain any PASS legs and finish the PENDING legs.
  - 2026-10-01 (loop batch-20261001b): DONE, AC-059 + AC-065 PASS. Causes: the match page had no way to point a row at an existing record or add one of the right kind (only Verify/Skip, raw ids); a group Verify/Skip ran at once; and every dataset treated a link with no record as "not imported yet", so a row a person skipped was made again on the next import. Built: convex/importResolution.ts (chooseExistingRecord, addRecordForItem for contact/company/venue rows, findRecordsForItem on the search indexes, itemRecordLabels), decision saved on the link via the generated link commands as the signed-in person (record, checked, decision approved, note; made snapshot cleared so a stopped import never takes back a picked record - importCancel also skips person-resolved links); convex/lib/importResolution.ts skippedByPerson used by all six dataset paths + companies; page shows old row name/details beside the Capsule record name, ResolveImportItem, BulkActionPreview. Decision - what happens to the record the import already made when a person picks another: (a) relink only, tell the person it is still there [chosen: no record is deleted or merged by a guess, events and later imports follow the picked record; negative: a duplicate stays until someone merges/archives it]; (b) merge it into the picked one automatically [one step; negative: merge is admin-only, moves history, only clients can merge, wrong pick is costly]; (c) archive it when untouched [cleaner; negative: needs per-type archive rules and can hide a record a person started using]. Decision - "create correctly typed": contact rows can be added as a person or a company, venue rows as a venue [chosen: these rows carry enough to make a full record]; event/lead/dish/pack-list rows only take Pick existing [negative: a failed event row with no date still needs its source fixed - inventing a date would be wrong]. Open: merging the left-behind duplicate stays a separate step (PL-SOURCE-MERGE); phone-size pass of the match page. No manifest change.

- [x] **P1 · PL-SOURCE-MERGE — Make merge and relink preserve dependent history.** Preserve source links and native field ownership while moving dependent records. Complete atomic or checkpointed correction with progress and failed-retry recovery; no orphaned communication, event, invoice or payroll evidence. Owners/reuse: `src/import/; src/sales/; convex/importCommit.ts; authored client-merge seam`. Acceptance: AC-061. Verify every named test/review on those rows; retain any PASS legs and finish the PENDING legs.
  - 2026-10-01 (loop batch-20261001b): DONE, AC-061 PASS. Causes: ClientMerge moved events, contacts (and so their communications), proposals, contracts, invoices, payments, payment methods and credit memos, but left leads, follow-up reminders and website quote requests on the hidden duplicate; and a later old-system change for the merged-away contact row was sent to the hidden duplicate (refused, turned into a review item). Built: stageClientMerge/reassignClient + merge fields on Lead, ClientOutreachTask, QuoteSubmission and fanOuts in contact-merge.manifest (regen); importSourceDelta follows survivingClientId for contacts (repeat import and Use new value). Payroll records hang on people, not clients, so a client merge cannot orphan them. Decision - failed-merge recovery: (a) keep the merge as one save [chosen: Convex runs the merge and every move in one transaction, so a failure leaves nothing half-done and a retry is the same merge; negative: a client with a very large history could hit the per-save size limit]; (b) checkpointed batches with a progress record [handles any size; negative: half-merged clients are visible between batches and need a resume screen - more to go wrong for a catering client list]. Decision - removed (soft-deleted) records on the duplicate stay where they are [no merge is blocked by old removed rows; negative: a removed lead still names the old client]. Open: progress display is not needed while the merge is one save; revisit only if a real client hits the size limit. Canonical port: the new merge fields/commands/fanOuts to Manifest-source.

- [x] **P1 · PL-SOURCE-HISTORY — Make imported communications and tasks normal readable history.** Materialize original timestamps/completion/source under the actual client/Event for search and navigation. Attachments alone do not meet the history workflow; importing never sends messages, reopens completed tasks or schedules anniversaries/reminders implicitly. Owners/reuse: `src/import/; src/features/clients/; src/features/workforce/; inbox/task owners`. Acceptance: AC-062, AC-111. Verify every named test/review on those rows; retain any PASS legs and finish the PENDING legs.
  - 2026-10-01 (loop batch-20261001b): DONE, AC-062 + AC-111 PASS. Cause: no import kind carried old messages or tasks at all, and client history (ClientCommunication) could only hang on a client contact or an event, while the contacts import makes person clients with no contact rows. Built: import kind "Messages and tasks" (ImportRun datasetType history; documented map TPP_HISTORY_MAPPINGS + field dispositions), reader convex/lib/tppHistoryRows.ts, seam convex/importHistory.ts (client via contacts/companies links followed through merges, event via events link; row + import link in one transaction; missing client/event -> waits on the match list, picked up by a later run), ClientCommunication clientId/importRunId/importedFrom/dueAt/completedAt/taskDone + note/task kinds + merge staging and fanOut; client page history shows client-level rows, "From the old system", "Written by", task state, and a Search history box. Proof: tests/proofs/import-communication-history.runtime.test.ts, tests/proofs/history-import-inert.runtime.test.ts. Decision - how old history is written: (a) seam insert with no Manifest event [chosen: an entity has one create step and `record` stamps the signed-in person as author, so a second create step would replace the person-facing one; no event means no reaction can ever send or remind; negative: the write is checked in the seam, not by a Manifest guard]; (b) change `record` to also take old-system fields [one path; negative: weakens the "author is the signed-in person" rule for every person-made note]; (c) a separate history entity [clean split; negative: two history lists to read, search and merge]. Decision - old open tasks: (a) kept as open lines in history, never reminders [chosen: AC-111 forbids implicit reminders; negative: a still-real old to-do must be re-made by a person]; (b) open a follow-up reminder [visible on the task list; negative: floods sales with years-old overdue reminders]. Decision - a done task with no done date shows "Done" (taskDone) rather than a made-up date. Open: the TPP export that carries contact history is not in the repo (column names follow the documented map; aliases ActivityID/ActivityDate/Type are read too); a stopped import does not take back history rows (history is inert; the rows stay listed on the run's links); phone-size browser pass of the client history after landing. Canonical port: ClientCommunication fields + merge commands, note/task kinds, ImportDatasetType history, CapsuleEntityType client_communication.

- [ ] **P1 · PL-DELIVERY-RECOVERY — Close ambiguous external-success and retry windows.** Use stable operation IDs and provider reconciliation after remote success/lost receipt (#383). Bound attempts/backoff, retain uncertain/dead-letter state and scoped retry. Deliberate new reminders stay possible; provider failures do not roll back unrelated business edits. Owners/reuse: `convex/qboSync.ts; convex/googleCalendar.ts; convex/invoiceReminders.ts; durable delivery owners`. Acceptance: AC-108, AC-118, AC-119, AC-163, AC-206, AC-207, AC-208, AC-346, AC-347, AC-348, AC-361, AC-373, AC-634. Verify every named test/review on those rows; retain any PASS legs and finish the PENDING legs.
  - 2026-10-01 (batch-20261001b): one delivery-state meaning for every outbound channel (convex/lib/deliveryState.ts: pending/processing/delivered/retryable_failed/terminal_failed/uncertain, growing waits, safe error class); webhooks claim each send first, carry one delivery id per event per endpoint, retry failures a later success passed, show "Will try again / Stopped / Not sure" with a manager Try again; text alerts claim each text (no double text, a lost answer is never re-sent); the sign-in email keeps a saved record shown on the team row; Integrations shows Outside messages counts. Decisions: a 4xx that will not fix itself stops at once (vs. 3 tries: fewer pointless sends, a person retries after fixing the address); a lost webhook send goes again with the same id (receiver drops it) while a lost text is never re-sent (a second text to a cook is worse than a missed one; the person can see "not sure"); the sign-in email retry stays the existing "Email sign-in again" button (vs. automatic retries: the scheduled job has no signed-in manager, and the invitation replaces itself safely). PASS: AC-163, 206, 208, 346, 347, 348, 361, 373, 634. Open: AC-119 QBO leg, AC-108 invoice-reminder leg, AC-118 Stripe - all paused by Ryan's 2026-09-29 no-money rule; AC-207 blocked: no provider sends webhooks to Capsule (Stripe and Google are polled), a provider webhook needs an outside account's signing secret.
  - 2026-10-07 (batch-20261007b): AC-108 PASS. The reminder leg was wrongly parked under the no-money rule (it is an email). Real fault: jsPDF stamps the clock and a random file id into every PDF, so a retry of a reminder the email service had already taken was a 'changed' email under the same key; the service answers 409 and Capsule recorded the client's address as refused (later reminders stopped). Fix: the reminder PDF is made from the reminder's fixed send time (same bytes every try); a 409 invalid_idempotent_request is recorded as 'taken on an earlier try, not sent again' (no email id, address stays good); a 409 concurrent request is a 'try again later'. Options: (A, chosen) stable bytes + treat a key conflict as sent - one email, worst case one reminder recorded as sent that only the service knows about / (B) new key on every retry - never a 409, but a lost answer sends the client a second email / (C) stop and ask staff - safe but every lost answer becomes office work. Proof: invoice-reminder-crash-reconcile 2/2 (fail without the fix), other reminder + invoice email proofs 5 files PASS, typecheck PASS. Still open: AC-118 / AC-119 QuickBooks + Stripe legs (no-money rule), AC-207 (signed inbound webhooks, issue #52).
  - 2026-10-07 (batch-20261007c): the manual sends (email the invoice, email the proposal, inbox reply, supplier order email) still called a 409 from the email service "check the client's email address". A 409 is about the send key, not the address: invalid_idempotent_request now says "The email service already took this email on an earlier try, so it was not sent again"; any other 409 says send again in a few minutes (convex/lib/reminderDelivery.ts, one helper for all four). Proof invoice-email-send 5/5 (new 409 case), proposal/reply/vendor/reminder email proofs 22/22 total, typecheck, format:check. Open unchanged: AC-118/119 (no-money rule), AC-207 (#52).

New convex/venueVendorPolicy.ts: rules in force on the event's date; strongest wins (banned > restricted > preferred > approved). Options for each state: block everything not preferred (bank-style, stops normal buying) vs block banned only and warn on restricted vs warn only (lets a banned vendor reach a venue that will turn them away at the door). Chose block banned / warn restricted: banned means the venue will not let them in, a real harm; restricted is a "check first". The block sits on the event's rental and purchase-order steps (proposals pick no vendors); a weekly order with no event is not a venue matter. A changed ban is fixed on the venue's vendor list, so live work stays correctable.   - 2026-10-02 (batch-20261002e, 43916b99 + 545eef43): AC-155 PASS (tests/proofs/scoped-deletion-holds.runtime.test.ts 1/1). New convex/personalDataErasure.ts (preview / erase / placeHold / releaseHold / history) + "Erase details" panel and history on Personal data exports; export path unchanged. Decision - what an erase does: (a) hard-delete every row about the person: full erasure, but destroys pay, shift and food safety history the spec says to keep; (b) erase contact/home details + personal scheduling notes, keep pay/hours/shifts/qualifications/food safety checks (and the name when such history exists), one audit row: keeps required history, still removes what identifies and reaches the person; (c) preview only, no erase until the owner sets periods: safest, but leaves an admin no way to honour a request. Chose (b). Holds: a per-person hold with a reason (ledger rows, no new entity) - benefit: a claim or dispute freezes everything with one click; negative: holds are not tied to a case record. Blocks only where real harm: on hold, still working (would break schedules), own record (loses sign-in), already erased. No time-based deletion: the owner has not set retention periods (spec open question), so kept records stay; no approval added to any ordinary edit. Open: browser pass after landing; tenant matrix not rerun (new reads/writes are admin + tenant checked).
